```
     1→# Memory
     2→
     3→## User Preferences
     4→
     5→- **Commit approval**: Always present the full commit commands to the user for physical review BEFORE executing them. Do not run jj commit / git commit until the user explicitly approves.
     6→- **Versioning**: Every plan must start with a version bump per notes/README.md "Versioning during development". Get user approval on single-step vs multi-step approach before starting.
     7→- **Pre-commit checklist**: Before committing, run: tests, clippy, fmt, install the app, then retest.
     8→
     9→## Feedback
    10→
    11→- [Always include ochid trailer in commits](feedback_ochid_trailer.md)
    12→- [Always commit .claude repo before finalize](feedback_claude_commit_before_finalize.md)
    13→- [Skip .claude push before finalize — finalize handles it](feedback_no_claude_push.md)
    14→- [Finalize must be absolute last action — only status line after](feedback_finalize_last.md)
    15→- [Use --ignore-immutable for late squash after push](feedback_squash_ignore_immutable.md)
    16→- [Use vc-x1 chid to retrieve changeIDs, not jj log](feedback_use_chid.md)
    17→- [Recognize when stuck — 5+ min thinking on simple task means stuck](feedback_stuck_detection.md)
    18→- [No --allow-new flag for jj git push](feedback_jj_no_allow_new.md)
    19→- [No text output after finalize — trailing writes miss squash window](feedback_no_finalize_echo.md)
    20→- [Ask for clarification on ambiguous input instead of spinning](feedback_ask_on_ambiguity.md)
    21→- ["finalize steps" triggers full session-end workflow](feedback_finalize_steps.md)
    22→- [Use relative paths in commands for brevity](feedback_relative_paths.md)
    23→- [Use -L for ochid retrieval — clean parseable output](feedback_use_no_label.md)
    24→
    25→## Lessons Learned
    26→
    27→- **Avoid duplicate commits in .claude repo**: Committing the bot session repo multiple times in one session can create duplicate-titled commits with other session commits interleaved, making squashing non-trivial. Prefer a single commit to .claude at session end.
    28→- **ochid trailers need updating after squash**: When squashing changes the target commit in .claude, the ochid reference in the app repo must be updated to point at the correct changeID.
    29→- **Don't modify .claude repo programmatically mid-session**: Session data updates continuously, so bot-initiated changes to .claude can create circular modifications.
    30→
    31→## Key Files
    32→
    33→- notes/README.md — contains versioning workflow, todo format rules
    34→- notes/todo.md — near-term task tracking
    35→- notes/chores-01.md — chore details and reference links
    36→- CLAUDE.md — dual repo commit/push workflow
    37→
```

```
     1→use std::path::PathBuf;
     2→use std::process::Command;
     3→use std::sync::Arc;
     4→
     5→use clap::Args;
     6→use jj_lib::object_id::ObjectId;
     7→use jj_lib::repo::{ReadonlyRepo, Repo};
     8→use jj_lib::workspace::Workspace;
     9→
    10→use crate::common;
    11→
    12→const DEFAULT_ID_LEN: usize = 12;
    13→
    14→#[derive(Args, Debug)]
    15→pub struct FixOchidArgs {
    16→    /// Revision (with optional .. notation)
    17→    #[arg(value_name = "REVISION")]
    18→    pub pos_rev: Option<String>,
    19→
    20→    /// Number of commits (per dotted side)
    21→    #[arg(value_name = "COMMITS")]
    22→    pub pos_count: Option<usize>,
    23→
    24→    /// Revision(s) to fix
    25→    #[arg(short, long, default_value = "@")]
    26→    pub revision: String,
    27→
    28→    /// Path to jj repo
    29→    #[arg(short = 'R', long, default_value = ".")]
    30→    pub repo: PathBuf,
    31→
    32→    /// Path to the other repo (e.g. .claude or .)
    33→    #[arg(long = "other-repo")]
    34→    pub other_repo: PathBuf,
    35→
    36→    /// Expected changeID length
    37→    #[arg(long = "id-len", default_value_t = DEFAULT_ID_LEN)]
    38→    pub id_len: usize,
    39→
    40→    /// New title to replace the first line (optional)
    41→    #[arg(long)]
    42→    pub title: Option<String>,
    43→
    44→    /// Fallback ochid value for IDs not found in other repo (e.g. /.claude/lost)
    45→    #[arg(long)]
    46→    pub fallback: Option<String>,
    47→
    48→    /// Actually write changes (default is dry-run)
    49→    #[arg(long = "no-dry-run")]
    50→    pub no_dry_run: bool,
    51→
    52→    /// Number of commits to fix
    53→    #[arg(short = 'n', long = "commits", value_name = "COMMITS")]
    54→    pub limit: Option<usize>,
    55→}
    56→
    57→/// Derive the ochid path prefix from a repo path.
    58→///
    59→/// `.` → `/`, `.claude` → `/.claude/`, `some/path` → `/some/path/`
    60→fn repo_to_ochid_prefix(repo_path: &std::path::Path) -> String {
    61→    let s = repo_path.to_string_lossy();
    62→    if s == "." {
    63→        "/".to_string()
    64→    } else {
    65→        let trimmed = s.trim_end_matches('/');
    66→        format!("/{trimmed}/")
    67→    }
    68→}
    69→
    70→/// Problems found with an ochid trailer.
    71→#[derive(Debug)]
    72→struct OchidIssues {
    73→    wrong_prefix: bool,
    74→    wrong_length: bool,
    75→    not_found: bool,
    76→}
    77→
    78→impl OchidIssues {
    79→    fn any(&self) -> bool {
    80→        self.wrong_prefix || self.wrong_length || self.not_found
    81→    }
    82→
    83→    fn summary(&self) -> String {
    84→        let mut parts = Vec::new();
    85→        if self.wrong_prefix {
    86→            parts.push("wrong prefix");
    87→        }
    88→        if self.wrong_length {
    89→            parts.push("wrong ID length");
    90→        }
    91→        if self.not_found {
    92→            parts.push("ID not found in other repo");
    93→        }
    94→        parts.join(", ")
    95→    }
    96→}
    97→
    98→/// Validate an ochid trailer value against expected prefix, length, and other repo.
    99→fn validate_ochid(
   100→    value: &str,
   101→    other_prefix: &str,
   102→    id_len: usize,
   103→    other_workspace: &Workspace,
   104→    other_repo: &Arc<ReadonlyRepo>,
   105→) -> OchidIssues {
   106→    let wrong_prefix = !value.starts_with(other_prefix);
   107→
   108→    // Extract the bare ID
   109→    let bare_id = if let Some(pos) = value.rfind('/') {
   110→        &value[pos + 1..]
   111→    } else {
   112→        value
   113→    };
   114→
   115→    let wrong_length = bare_id.len() != id_len;
   116→
   117→    // Check if the ID resolves in the other repo
   118→    let not_found = common::resolve_revset(other_workspace, other_repo, bare_id)
   119→        .map(|ids| ids.is_empty())
   120→        .unwrap_or(true);
   121→
   122→    OchidIssues {
   123→        wrong_prefix,
   124→        wrong_length,
   125→        not_found,
   126→    }
   127→}
   128→
   129→/// Fix the ochid trailer in a commit description.
   130→fn fix_ochid_in_description(
   131→    desc: &str,
   132→    other_prefix: &str,
   133→    id_len: usize,
   134→    new_title: Option<&str>,
   135→) -> String {
   136→    let mut lines: Vec<String> = desc.lines().map(|l| l.to_string()).collect();
   137→
   138→    // Fix title if requested
   139→    if let Some(title) = new_title
   140→        && !lines.is_empty()
   141→    {
   142→        lines[0] = title.to_string();
   143→    }
   144→
   145→    // Fix ochid trailer
   146→    for line in &mut lines {
   147→        let trimmed = line.trim();
   148→        if let Some(value) = trimmed.strip_prefix("ochid:") {
   149→            let value = value.trim();
   150→            // Extract the bare changeID (strip any existing path prefix)
   151→            let bare_id = if let Some(pos) = value.rfind('/') {
   152→                &value[pos + 1..]
   153→            } else {
   154→                value
   155→            };
   156→            // Normalize to id_len chars
   157→            let short_id = &bare_id[..bare_id.len().min(id_len)];
   158→            *line = format!("ochid: {other_prefix}{short_id}");
   159→        }
   160→    }
   161→
   162→    // Preserve trailing newline if original had one
   163→    let mut result = lines.join("\n");
   164→    if desc.ends_with('\n') {
   165→        result.push('\n');
   166→    }
   167→    result
   168→}
   169→
   170→pub fn fix_ochid(args: &FixOchidArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
   171→    let (workspace, repo) = common::load_repo(&args.repo)?;
   172→    let (other_workspace, other_repo) = common::load_repo(&args.other_repo)?;
   173→    let other_prefix = repo_to_ochid_prefix(&args.other_repo);
   174→
   175→    let spec = common::resolve_spec(
   176→        args.pos_rev.as_deref(),
   177→        args.pos_count,
   178→        &args.revision,
   179→        args.limit,
   180→        "@",
   181→    );
   182→
   183→    let (ids, _anchor_index) = common::collect_ids(
   184→        &workspace,
   185→        &repo,
   186→        &spec.rev,
   187→        spec.desc_count,
   188→        spec.anc_count,
   189→    )?;
   190→    if ids.is_empty() {
   191→        return Err(format!("no commits found for revision '{}'", args.revision).into());
   192→    }
   193→
   194→    let root_id = repo.store().root_commit_id().clone();
   195→    let mut fixed = 0;
   196→    let mut skipped = 0;
   197→    let mut errors = 0;
   198→
   199→    for commit_id in &ids {
   200→        if *commit_id == root_id {
   201→            continue;
   202→        }
   203→        let commit = repo.store().get_commit(commit_id)?;
   204→        let desc = commit.description();
   205→        let change_hex = jj_lib::hex_util::encode_reverse_hex(commit.change_id().as_bytes());
   206→        let change_short = &change_hex[..change_hex.len().min(12)];
   207→
   208→        // Extract current ochid value
   209→        let current_ochid = common::extract_ochid(&commit);
   210→
   211→        // Validate current ochid
   212→        let issues = if let Some(ref ochid_val) = current_ochid {
   213→            validate_ochid(
   214→                ochid_val,
   215→                &other_prefix,
   216→                args.id_len,
   217→                &other_workspace,
   218→                &other_repo,
   219→            )
   220→        } else {
   221→            // No ochid trailer — nothing to fix
   222→            skipped += 1;
   223→            if !args.no_dry_run {
   224→                println!("skip {change_short}  (no ochid trailer)");
   225→            }
   226→            continue;
   227→        };
   228→
   229→        if !issues.any() {
   230→            skipped += 1;
   231→            if !args.no_dry_run {
   232→                println!("ok   {change_short}  (valid)");
   233→            }
   234→            continue;
   235→        }
   236→
   237→        // Build the fixed description
   238→        let mut new_desc =
   239→            fix_ochid_in_description(desc, &other_prefix, args.id_len, args.title.as_deref());
   240→
   241→        // Re-extract the fixed ochid value for post-validation
   242→        let mut fixed_ochid = {
   243→            let mut val = None;
   244→            for line in new_desc.lines() {
   245→                let trimmed = line.trim();
   246→                if let Some(v) = trimmed.strip_prefix("ochid:") {
   247→                    val = Some(v.trim().to_string());
   248→                }
   249→            }
   250→            val
   251→        };
   252→
   253→        let post_issues = if let Some(ref v) = fixed_ochid {
   254→            validate_ochid(v, &other_prefix, args.id_len, &other_workspace, &other_repo)
   255→        } else {
   256→            OchidIssues {
   257→                wrong_prefix: false,
   258→                wrong_length: false,
   259→                not_found: true,
   260→            }
   261→        };
   262→
   263→        if post_issues.not_found {
   264→            if let Some(ref fallback) = args.fallback {
   265→                // Replace the ochid line with the fallback value
   266→                let mut fb_lines: Vec<String> = new_desc.lines().map(|l| l.to_string()).collect();
   267→                for line in &mut fb_lines {
   268→                    if line.trim().starts_with("ochid:") {
   269→                        *line = format!("ochid: {fallback}");
   270→                    }
   271→                }
   272→                let fb_desc = if new_desc.ends_with('\n') {
   273→                    format!("{}\n", fb_lines.join("\n"))
   274→                } else {
   275→                    fb_lines.join("\n")
   276→                };
   277→                new_desc = fb_desc;
   278→                fixed_ochid = Some(fallback.clone());
   279→            } else {
   280→                errors += 1;
   281→                eprintln!(
   282→                    "err  {change_short}  ID not found in other repo (ochid: {})",
   283→                    fixed_ochid.as_deref().unwrap_or("?")
   284→                );
   285→                continue;
   286→            }
   287→        }
   288→
   289→        if !args.no_dry_run {
   290→            let first_line = new_desc.lines().next().unwrap_or("");
   291→            println!("fix  {change_short}  {first_line}  [{}]", issues.summary());
   292→            if let Some(ref v) = fixed_ochid {
   293→                println!("     -> ochid: {v}");
   294→            }
   295→        } else {
   296→            let status = Command::new("jj")
   297→                .arg("describe")
   298→                .arg("-m")
   299→                .arg(&new_desc)
   300→                .arg("-r")
   301→                .arg(commit_id.hex())
   302→                .arg("-R")
   303→                .arg(&args.repo)
   304→                .arg("--ignore-immutable")
   305→                .stdout(std::process::Stdio::null())
   306→                .stderr(std::process::Stdio::inherit())
   307→                .status()?;
   308→            if !status.success() {
   309→                return Err(format!(
   310→                    "jj describe failed for {} (exit {})",
   311→                    change_short,
   312→                    status.code().unwrap_or(-1)
   313→                )
   314→                .into());
   315→            }
   316→            let first_line = new_desc.lines().next().unwrap_or("");
   317→            println!("fixed {change_short}  {first_line}");
   318→        }
   319→        fixed += 1;
   320→    }
   321→
   322→    println!(
   323→        "\n{fixed} fixed, {skipped} skipped, {errors} errors (of {} total)",
   324→        ids.len()
   325→    );
   326→    if errors > 0 {
   327→        Err(format!("{errors} commit(s) could not be fixed").into())
   328→    } else {
   329→        Ok(())
   330→    }
   331→}
   332→
   333→#[cfg(test)]
   334→mod tests {
   335→    use super::*;
   336→
   337→    #[test]
   338→    fn prefix_from_dot() {
   339→        assert_eq!(repo_to_ochid_prefix(&PathBuf::from(".")), "/");
   340→    }
   341→
```

> AGENT

I see the bug. On line 173, the prefix is derived from `--other-repo` path:

```rust
let other_prefix = repo_to_ochid_prefix(&args.other_repo);
```

When `--other-repo ../` is passed, `repo_to_ochid_prefix` hits the `else` branch and produces `/..//` (after trimming the trailing slash: `/../`). But the ochid prefix should represent where the **other repo** lives relative to the **workspace root**, not the raw CLI path.

The ochid convention is:
- App repo commits → `/.claude/<id>` (other repo is `.claude`)
- `.claude` commits → `/<id>` (other repo is `.` i.e. workspace root)

So `repo_to_ochid_prefix` needs to canonicalize the path. When you pass `../` from inside `.claude`, that resolves to the workspace root, which should produce prefix `/`.

The fix: canonicalize `other_repo` relative to `repo`, then compute the prefix from the canonical relative path. Or more simply, canonicalize both paths and compute the relative path from workspace root.

Let me look at how the ochid prefix is actually supposed to work — it's the path from workspace root to the other repo.

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/src/fix_ochid.rs",
  "old_string": "/// Derive the ochid path prefix from a repo path.\n///\n/// `.` → `/`, `.claude` → `/.claude/`, `some/path` → `/some/path/`\nfn repo_to_ochid_prefix(repo_path: &std::path::Path) -> String {\n    let s = repo_path.to_string_lossy();\n    if s == \".\" {\n        \"/\".to_string()\n    } else {\n        let trimmed = s.trim_end_matches('/');\n        format!(\"/{trimmed}/\")\n    }\n}",
  "new_string": "/// Derive the ochid path prefix from the repo and other-repo paths.\n///\n/// The prefix is the workspace-root-relative path to the other repo.\n/// We canonicalize both paths to resolve `..` components, then compute\n/// the other repo's path relative to the workspace root (app repo parent).\n///\n/// Examples (from workspace root):\n///   repo=`.`, other=`.claude`     → `/.claude/`\n///   repo=`.claude`, other=`..`    → `/`\n///   repo=`.`, other=`.`           → `/`\nfn compute_ochid_prefix(repo_path: &std::path::Path, other_repo_path: &std::path::Path) -> Result<String, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    let repo_canon = std::fs::canonicalize(repo_path)\n        .map_err(|e| format!(\"cannot canonicalize repo path '{}': {e}\", repo_path.display()))?;\n    let other_canon = std::fs::canonicalize(other_repo_path)\n        .map_err(|e| format!(\"cannot canonicalize other-repo path '{}': {e}\", other_repo_path.display()))?;\n\n    // The workspace root is the app repo. If -R points to a subdirectory\n    // (like .claude), the workspace root is its parent. We determine this\n    // by finding the common ancestor: the workspace root is always the\n    // shorter (or equal) of the two canonical paths, assuming one is a\n    // subdirectory of the other or they are the same.\n    //\n    // Simpler approach: compute other_canon relative to repo_canon's parent\n    // (if repo is a subdir) or repo_canon itself (if repo is the root).\n    // Actually, the ochid prefix is always relative to the workspace root,\n    // and the workspace root is the *outermost* of the two repos.\n\n    // Find the workspace root: whichever canonical path is a prefix of the other\n    // (or they're the same, meaning workspace root = both).\n    let workspace_root = if other_canon.starts_with(&repo_canon) {\n        &repo_canon\n    } else if repo_canon.starts_with(&other_canon) {\n        &other_canon\n    } else {\n        return Err(format!(\n            \"repos are not nested: '{}' and '{}'\",\n            repo_canon.display(),\n            other_canon.display()\n        ).into());\n    };\n\n    let relative = other_canon.strip_prefix(workspace_root)\n        .map_err(|e| format!(\"cannot compute relative path: {e}\"))?;\n\n    let rel_str = relative.to_string_lossy();\n    if rel_str.is_empty() {\n        Ok(\"/\".to_string())\n    } else {\n        Ok(format!(\"/{rel_str}/\"))\n    }\n}"
}
```

```json
{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/src/toml_simple.rs",
  "content": "use std::collections::HashMap;\nuse std::path::Path;\n\n/// Load a TOML file into a flat key-value map.\n///\n/// Handles `[section]` headers, bare `key = \"value\"` pairs, comments, and\n/// blank lines. Keys under a section are stored as `section.key`. Quoted\n/// string values have their quotes stripped; unquoted values are stored\n/// as-is.\n///\n/// This is intentionally minimal — just enough for `.vc-config.toml`.\npub fn toml_load(path: &Path) -> Result<HashMap<String, String>, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    let content = std::fs::read_to_string(path)\n        .map_err(|e| format!(\"cannot read '{}': {e}\", path.display()))?;\n\n    let mut map = HashMap::new();\n    let mut section = String::new();\n\n    for line in content.lines() {\n        let trimmed = line.trim();\n\n        // Skip blank lines and comments\n        if trimmed.is_empty() || trimmed.starts_with('#') {\n            continue;\n        }\n\n        // Section header: [name]\n        if let Some(inner) = trimmed.strip_prefix('[').and_then(|s| s.strip_suffix(']')) {\n            section = inner.trim().to_string();\n            continue;\n        }\n\n        // Key = value\n        if let Some((key, value)) = trimmed.split_once('=') {\n            let key = key.trim();\n            let value = value.trim();\n\n            // Strip surrounding quotes from string values\n            let value = if (value.starts_with('\"') && value.ends_with('\"'))\n                || (value.starts_with('\\'') && value.ends_with('\\''))\n            {\n                &value[1..value.len() - 1]\n            } else {\n                value\n            };\n\n            let full_key = if section.is_empty() {\n                key.to_string()\n            } else {\n                format!(\"{section}.{key}\")\n            };\n\n            map.insert(full_key, value.to_string());\n        }\n    }\n\n    Ok(map)\n}\n\n/// Look up a dotted key (e.g. `\"workspace.path\"`) in a loaded config map.\npub fn toml_get<'a>(map: &'a HashMap<String, String>, key: &str) -> Option<&'a String> {\n    map.get(key)\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n\n    #[test]\n    fn parse_section_and_key() {\n        let dir = std::env::temp_dir().join(\"toml_simple_test\");\n        std::fs::create_dir_all(&dir).unwrap();\n        let path = dir.join(\"test.toml\");\n        std::fs::write(\n            &path,\n            \"# comment\\n\\n[workspace]\\npath = \\\"/\\\"\\n\",\n        )\n        .unwrap();\n\n        let map = toml_load(&path).unwrap();\n        assert_eq!(toml_get(&map, \"workspace.path\"), Some(&\"/\".to_string()));\n        std::fs::remove_dir_all(&dir).ok();\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn parse_quoted_value() {\n        let dir = std::env::temp_dir().join(\"toml_simple_quoted\");\n        std::fs::create_dir_all(&dir).unwrap();\n        let path = dir.join(\"test.toml\");\n        std::fs::write(&path, \"[section]\\nkey = \\\"value\\\"\\n\").unwrap();\n\n        let map = toml_load(&path).unwrap();\n        assert_eq!(toml_get(&map, \"section.key\"), Some(&\"value\".to_string()));\n        std::fs::remove_dir_all(&dir).ok();\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn bare_key_no_section() {\n        let dir = std::env::temp_dir().join(\"toml_simple_bare\");\n        std::fs::create_dir_all(&dir).unwrap();\n        let path = dir.join(\"test.toml\");\n        std::fs::write(&path, \"name = \\\"hello\\\"\\n\").unwrap();\n\n        let map = toml_load(&path).unwrap();\n        assert_eq!(toml_get(&map, \"name\"), Some(&\"hello\".to_string()));\n        std::fs::remove_dir_all(&dir).ok();\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn missing_file_errors() {\n        let path = std::path::PathBuf::from(\"/nonexistent/test.toml\");\n        assert!(toml_load(&path).is_err());\n    }\n}\n"
}
```

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_01V8AnHCFccru6PmhWxRqVnb
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/src/fix_ochid.rs",
  "old_string": "/// Derive the ochid path prefix from a repo path.\n///\n/// `.` → `/`, `.claude` → `/.claude/`, `some/path` → `/some/path/`\nfn repo_to_ochid_prefix(repo_path: &std::path::Path) -> String {\n    let s = repo_path.to_string_lossy();\n    if s == \".\" {\n        \"/\".to_string()\n    } else {\n        let trimmed = s.trim_end_matches('/');\n        format!(\"/{trimmed}/\")\n    }\n}",
  "new_string": "/// Derive the ochid prefix from the other repo's `.vc-config.toml`.\n///\n/// The `workspace.path` value is the repo's path relative to the workspace\n/// root, which is exactly the ochid prefix (with a trailing `/` when the\n/// path isn't just `/`).\nfn ochid_prefix_from_config(\n    config: &std::collections::HashMap<String, String>,\n) -> Result<String, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    let ws_path = toml_simple::toml_get(config, \"workspace.path\")\n        .ok_or(\"missing workspace.path in .vc-config.toml\")?;\n    let trimmed = ws_path.trim_end_matches('/');\n    if trimmed.is_empty() {\n        Ok(\"/\".to_string())\n    } else {\n        Ok(format!(\"{trimmed}/\"))\n    }\n}"
}
```

> AGENT

All green. Here's a summary of what changed:

**Bug**: `fix-ochid` computed the ochid prefix from the raw `--other-repo` CLI path (e.g. `../` → `/../`), instead of resolving it to a workspace-relative path.

**Fix** (0.22.1):
- **New module `toml_simple.rs`**: `toml_load()` parses a TOML file into a `HashMap<String, String>` (loaded once), `toml_get()` looks up a key.
- **`fix_ochid.rs`**: Replaced `repo_to_ochid_prefix(path)` with `ochid_prefix_from_config(config)` which reads `workspace.path` from the other repo's `.vc-config.toml` — the authoritative source for the prefix.
- Tests updated to match.

Ready for commit whenever you are.

     1→# Chores-02
     2→
     3→## Graphical tools for jj repos
     4→
     5→### GG (gg)
     6→
     7→Cross-platform GUI for jj (Tauri-based). Interactive graph view.
     8→
     9→- Install: https://github.com/gulbanana/gg
    10→- `gg gui` does NOT work; use `gg web` instead (opens in browser)
    11→
    12→### jj-fzf
    13→
    14→fzf-based TUI centered on `jj log` graph. Previews diffs, evolog,
    15→op log. Many keybindings for common jj operations.
    16→
    17→- Install: https://github.com/tim-janik/jj-fzf
    18→- Requires `fzf` installed separately (`sudo pacman -S fzf` on Arch)
    19→
    20→### Others (not tested)
    21→
    22→- **jjui** — TUI for jj (https://github.com/idursun/jjui)
    23→- **jjgui** — commercial desktop app (https://jj-gui.com/)
    24→- **Jujutsu Kaizen** — VS Code plugin
    25→- **VisualJJ** — VS Code plugin (native jj, no git colocation)
    26→- **Selvejj** — JetBrains plugin
    27→
    28→### Built-in
    29→
    30→- `jj log` — shows graph by default (no `--graph` flag needed)
    31→- `gitk --all` — works on the colocated `.git` but sees raw git refs,
    32→  not jj's view of history
    33→
    34→## jj commit organization and traversal mechanisms (0.17.0)
    35→
    36→### Motivation
    37→
    38→Understanding how jj commits are organized and what jj-lib APIs exist
    39→for traversing them — a prerequisite for building richer views of
    40→codebase evolution over time. The todo item "determine the organization
    41→of commits in jj and how we can iterate over them" prompted this
    42→investigation.
    43→
    44→### Three axes of traversal
    45→
    46→jj provides three distinct ways to navigate commit history:
    47→
    48→1. **Commit DAG** — parent/child relationships between commits
    49→2. **Refs/bookmarks** — named pointers (bookmarks, tags, git refs) into the DAG
    50→3. **Evolution log (evolog)** — rewrite history of a single ChangeId across operations
    51→
    52→Each answers a different question:
    53→- DAG: "what is the commit history?"
    54→- Refs: "where are the named branches pointing?"
    55→- Evolog: "how was this particular change built up?"
    56→
    57→### Commit DAG traversal via revsets
    58→
    59→jj-lib's revset engine supports both string-based and programmatic queries.
    60→
    61→**String-based** (what vc-x1 uses today via `common::resolve_revset`):
    62→```rust
    63→resolve_revset(&workspace, &repo, "::@")?  // ancestors of @
    64→resolve_revset(&workspace, &repo, "all()")? // all visible commits
    65→```
    66→
    67→**Programmatic** (builder API, no parsing needed):
    68→```rust
    69→use jj_lib::revset::RevsetExpression;
    70→
    71→RevsetExpression::all()                    // all visible + referenced
    72→RevsetExpression::visible_heads()          // tips of visible branches
    73→RevsetExpression::symbol("@".into())       // working copy
    74→RevsetExpression::commits(vec![id])        // explicit set
    75→RevsetExpression::bookmarks(pattern)       // bookmark refs
    76→RevsetExpression::git_refs()               // all git refs
    77→
    78→// Chainable traversal
    79→expr.ancestors()           // ::x
    80→expr.descendants()         // x::
    81→expr.parents()             // direct parents
    82→expr.children()            // direct children
    83→expr.heads()               // maximal elements
    84→expr.roots()               // minimal elements
    85→expr.fork_point()          // merge base
    86→
    87→// Ranges and set operations
    88→expr1.range(&expr2)        // x..y
    89→expr1.dag_range_to(&expr2) // x::y
    90→expr1.union(&expr2)
    91→expr1.intersection(&expr2)
    92→expr1.minus(&expr2)
    93→```
    94→
    95→### Iteration modes
    96→
    97→Evaluated revsets support three iteration modes:
    98→
    99→```rust
   100→let revset = expr.evaluate(repo)?;
   101→
   102→revset.iter()               // yields CommitId in topo order (newest first)
   103→revset.commit_change_ids()  // yields (CommitId, ChangeId) pairs
   104→revset.iter_graph()         // yields GraphNode<CommitId> with edge info
   105→```
   106→
   107→`iter_graph()` provides `GraphEdge` data with edge types (`Direct`,
   108→`Indirect`, `Missing`) — needed for rendering graph views like
   109→`jj log --graph`.
   110→
   111→### Ref/bookmark access via View
   112→
   113→```rust
   114→let view = repo.view();
   115→view.heads()              // HashSet<CommitId> — visible branch tips
   116→view.bookmarks()          // all bookmarks with local+remote state
   117→view.local_bookmarks()    // local bookmarks only
   118→view.git_refs()           // BTreeMap of git refs
   119→view.git_head()           // git HEAD
   120→```
   121→
   122→### Index (direct DAG queries)
   123→
   124→```rust
   125→let index = repo.index();
   126→index.is_ancestor(&id1, &id2)?           // ancestry check
   127→index.common_ancestors(&[id1], &[id2])?  // merge base
   128→index.heads(&mut candidates)?            // compute heads
   129→index.all_heads_for_gc()?                // every head including hidden
   130→```
   131→
   132→### Low-level DAG walks (dag_walk module)
   133→
   134→Generic traversal functions on arbitrary DAGs:
   135→
   136→```rust
   137→jj_lib::dag_walk::dfs(start, id_fn, neighbors_fn)
   138→jj_lib::dag_walk::topo_order_forward(...)
   139→jj_lib::dag_walk::topo_order_reverse(...)
   140→jj_lib::dag_walk::heads(...)
   141→jj_lib::dag_walk::closest_common_node(...)
   142→```
   143→
   144→### Evolution log (evolog)
   145→
   146→The evolog shows every version a ChangeId went through — snapshots,
   147→squashes, rebases, describes. It's tracked at the **operation level**,
   148→not in the commit DAG.
   149→
   150→Example output from `jj evolog -r @-`:
   151→```
   152→◆    rpyrwwnw wink@saville.com 2026-03-17 09:53:12 main f7f567f6
   153→├─╮  Finalize: replace --foreground with --detach, ...
   154→│ │  -- operation 655ff50a3386 squash commits into ...
   155→│ ○  tluyvppo/0 wink@saville.com 2026-03-17 09:53:12 89b1c104 (hidden)
   156→│ │  (no description set)
   157→│ │  -- operation e71fa874e37a snapshot working copy
   158→│ ○  tluyvppo/1 wink@saville.com 2026-03-17 09:49:54 317cefcf (hidden)
   159→│    (empty) (no description set)
   160→│    -- operation e1fe385617e5 commit ...
   161→○  rpyrwwnw/1 wink@saville.com 2026-03-17 09:49:54 50c30496 (hidden)
   162→│  Finalize: replace --foreground with --detach, ...
   163→...
   164→```
   165→
   166→**Core API** (`jj_lib::evolution`):
   167→
   168→```rust
   169→use jj_lib::evolution::{walk_predecessors, CommitEvolutionEntry};
   170→
   171→// Walk the rewrite history of a commit
   172→let iter = walk_predecessors(repo, &[commit_id]);
   173→for entry in iter {
   174→    let entry: CommitEvolutionEntry = entry?;
   175→    // entry.commit        — the commit at this point in history
   176→    // entry.operation      — the operation that created this version
   177→    // entry.predecessor_ids() — IDs of the previous version(s)
   178→    // entry.predecessors()    — iterator over predecessor Commit objects
   179→}
   180→
   181→// Batch resolve predecessors across operation ranges
   182→let map: BTreeMap<CommitId, Vec<CommitId>> =
   183→    accumulate_predecessors(new_ops, old_ops)?;
   184→```
   185→
   186→### Evolog data is local-only
   187→
   188→**Critical finding**: operation/evolution data does NOT survive a git
   189→push + clone cycle. This is by design.
   190→
   191→From `op_store.rs`:
   192→> "Operations and views are not meant to be exchanged between repos or
   193→> users; they represent local state and history."
   194→
   195→| Data                  | Pushed? | Recoverable on clone? |
   196→|-----------------------|---------|----------------------|
   197→| Git commits + trees   | Yes     | Yes                  |
   198→| Change IDs            | No      | Yes (from git header)|
   199→| Commit predecessors   | No      | No                   |
   200→| Operation history     | No      | No                   |
   201→| Evolution log         | No      | No                   |
   202→
   203→Predecessor tracking is stored per-operation in
   204→`op_store::Operation::commit_predecessors` (since jj 0.30). The old
   205→`backend::Commit::predecessors` field is deprecated and slated for
   206→removal around jj 0.42.
   207→
   208→### Implications for codebase evolution story
   209→
   210→The evolog provides a rich local narrative of how changes were built
   211→up — intermediate snapshots, false starts, squashes — that commit
   212→messages alone don't capture. However, this data is ephemeral across
   213→git transport.
   214→
   215→For the dual-repo workflow (app + `.claude`), the `.claude` repo's
   216→evolog captures how session commits were constructed. If `.claude`
   217→eventually becomes a git submodule or is backed by a database, the
   218→evolog data could be extracted and persisted via `walk_predecessors()`
   219→before it's lost to a clone. This would preserve the full provenance
   220→chain: not just what changed, but how the change was assembled.
   221→
   222→## Interactive DAG navigation: the missing feature (2026-03-18)
   223→
   224→### The problem
   225→
   226→When working with jj-git repos, especially complex ones, you need to
   227→navigate the commit DAG interactively — click a parent or child and
   228→jump to it instantly. This is table-stakes in gitk but absent from
   229→jj-native tooling.
   230→
   231→### gitk: still the best for DAG traversal
   232→
   233→gitk's killer feature: the parent/child list lets you **click-to-jump**
   234→to any commit, even if it's far off-screen. True graph traversal, not
   235→scrolling. Works in colocated jj repos via `jj git export`.
   236→
   237→**Downsides:** Shows Git's view only — no change IDs, no revsets, no
   238→jj bookmarks. Detached HEAD display is confusing. Cannot perform jj
   239→operations. Does not work with non-colocated jj repos.
   240→
   241→### gg-cli: close but not there
   242→
   243→Renders a true graphical DAG. Can show parents in the detail pane and
   244→click to select a parent — but **only if that parent is already visible
   245→in the left panel**. If the parent is off-screen, there is no jump-to
   246→behavior; you must manually scroll using the slider. This makes it
   247→**useless on large/complex repos** for DAG traversal.
   248→
   249→Drag-and-drop rebase is nice, but the lack of click-to-jump is a
   250→dealbreaker for navigation.
   251→
   252→### Other tools: list navigators, not graph navigators
   253→
   254→- **jj-fzf** — displays `jj log` graph in fzf with live revset editing.
   255→  Navigation is list-based (up/down), not graph-based (follow edges).
   256→- **jjui** — revision tree view, growing fast, but still fundamentally
   257→  a list navigator with a graph drawn alongside.
   258→- **lazyjj** — operations-focused TUI, not DAG-focused.
   259→- **jjdag** — pre-alpha, explicitly DAG-focused (Magit-style keys,
   260→  foldable tree). Worth watching.
   261→- **jj log** — revset language is powerful for querying (`children(x)`,
   262→  `ancestors(main..@)`) but output is static, not interactive.
   263→
   264→### Comparison
   265→
   266→| Tool     | DAG display | Click-to-jump parent/child | Native jj |
   267→|----------|-------------|---------------------------|-----------|
   268→| gitk     | graphical   | **yes** (both directions)  | no        |
   269→| gg-cli   | graphical   | parent only, no jump if off-screen | yes |
   270→| jj-fzf   | ASCII       | no                         | yes       |
   271→| jjui     | ASCII       | no                         | yes       |
   272→| jj log   | ASCII       | n/a (not interactive)      | yes       |
   273→
   274→### Conclusion
   275→
   276→**gitk remains the best tool for interactive DAG traversal** despite
   277→its jj blindspots. The jj ecosystem is missing a tool that combines
   278→jj-native concepts (change IDs, revsets, bookmarks) with gitk-style
   279→click-to-jump on parent/child links. This would be a strong feature
   280→request for gg-cli, which already has the infrastructure but lacks the
   281→jump-to behavior.
   282→
   283→### Cautionary tale: multi-tool dangers
   284→
   285→While testing jj-fzf and gg-cli simultaneously (with Zed editor also
   286→open), it's easy to accidentally move `@` to an unexpected commit via
   287→one tool without noticing. When Zed then sees files disappear from the
   288→working copy, closing a "changed" file and declining to save effectively
   289→deletes it. Recovery: `jj new main` to repoint `@`, or
   290→`jj file show <path> -r <rev> --at-op <op>` to extract from history.
   291→
   292→## jj-lib Commit API and tree storage model (2026-03-18)
   293→
   294→### What's on the Commit type
   295→
   296→Given a `Commit` (from `repo.store().get_commit(&commit_id)?`), the
   297→public API includes:
   298→
   299→| Method | Returns | Cost |
   300→|--------|---------|------|
   301→| `id()` | `&CommitId` | zero — stored in struct |
   302→| `change_id()` | `&ChangeId` | zero |
   303→| `description()` | `&str` | zero |
   304→| `author()` / `committer()` | `&Signature` | zero |
   305→| `parent_ids()` | `&[CommitId]` | zero — stored in commit data |
   306→| `parents()` | `Vec<Commit>` | async, loads parent commits |
   307→| `tree()` | `MergedTree` | near-zero — wraps tree IDs, no I/O |
   308→| `tree_ids()` | `&Merge<TreeId>` | zero |
   309→| `parent_tree(repo)` | `MergedTree` | async, merges parent trees |
   310→| `is_empty(repo)` | `bool` | compares tree to parent tree |
   311→| `has_conflict()` | `bool` | checks if tree IDs are conflicted |
   312→
   313→### What's NOT on Commit
   314→
   315→- **Children** — parent→child is a reverse lookup. Requires revsets:
   316→  ```rust
   317→  resolve_revset(&workspace, &repo, &format!("children({})", change_id))?
   318→  ```
   319→  Parents are stored in the commit; children require scanning the DAG.
   320→
   321→- **Diff/changes** — no method returns "what changed in this commit."
   322→  Must be computed by comparing two tree snapshots.
   323→
   324→### Getting diffs between a commit and its parent
   325→
   326→```rust
   327→use jj_lib::matchers::EverythingMatcher;
   328→
   329→let parent_tree = commit.parent_tree(repo).await?;
   330→let commit_tree = commit.tree();
   331→
   332→let mut diff_stream = parent_tree.diff_stream(&commit_tree, &EverythingMatcher);
   333→while let Some(entry) = diff_stream.next().await {
   334→    // entry.path  — RepoPathBuf (the file that changed)
   335→    // entry.values — Diff { before, after } of MergedTreeValue
   336→}
   337→```
   338→
   339→`parent_tree()` handles merge commits by merging multiple parent trees.
   340→
   341→### What's stored on disk
   342→
   343→jj uses git as its backend. On disk: git's content-addressable objects.
   344→
   345→- **Blobs** — file contents, deduplicated by SHA
   346→- **Trees** — directory listings (pointers to blobs and sub-trees)
   347→- **Commits** — pointer to root tree + parent IDs + metadata
   348→
   349→Each commit points to a **full tree snapshot**, but the tree is composed
   350→of shared objects. If a file didn't change, both commits' trees point to
   351→the same blob. Git also delta-compresses objects in packfiles.
   352→
   353→The "full snapshot" is **logical, not physical**. `commit.tree()` just
   354→wraps the root tree hash — no I/O happens until you traverse.
   355→
   356→### Cost model for tree operations
   357→
   358→| Operation | Cost | Why |
   359→|-----------|------|-----|
   360→| `commit.tree()` | near zero | wraps tree IDs, no I/O |
   361→| `tree.path_value(&path)` | O(path depth) | walks ~4-5 tree nodes |
   362→| `tree.diff_stream(&other)` | O(changed subtrees) | skips identical subtrees by hash |
   363→| `tree.entries()` | O(entire repo) | walks everything — avoid on large repos |
   364→
   365→The diff algorithm compares tree entries level by level. If a subtree
   366→hash matches, it's skipped entirely — never descended into. On a Linux
   367→monorepo commit that touches 5 files, it loads ~20-30 tree objects out
   368→of millions.
   369→
   370→The expensive part of a gitk-style patch view is reading **blob
   371→contents** for the line-by-line diff, but only for changed files.
   372→
   373→### jj-lib vs gitk efficiency
   374→
   375→jj-lib is a separate Rust implementation reading the same `.git/objects/`
   376→and packfiles — it does NOT shell out to git or link libgit2.
   377→
   378→The tree diff uses the **same hash-based short-circuiting** as git:
   379→`TreeDiffIterator` (in `merged_tree.rs`) checks `is_changed()` on
   380→`MergedTreeValue` (which compares tree hashes) and only recurses into
   381→directories that differ.
   382→
   383→jj-lib also has a feature gitk lacks: **concurrent tree loading**. When
   384→`store.concurrency() > 1`, it uses `TreeDiffStreamImpl` with async
   385→parallel I/O for loading tree objects, potentially faster than gitk on
   386→large repos.
   387→
   388→Source: `jj-lib-0.39.0/src/merged_tree.rs`, lines 260-690.
   389→
   390→### Key jj-lib source files (0.39.0)
   391→
   392→- `src/revset.rs` — revset expression types, builders, parsing
   393→- `src/evolution.rs` — evolog/predecessor traversal
   394→- `src/graph.rs` — graph structures, TopoGroupedGraphIterator
   395→- `src/dag_walk.rs` — generic DAG traversal (DFS, topo sort)
   396→- `src/repo.rs` — Repo trait, ReadonlyRepo
   397→- `src/view.rs` — View (heads, bookmarks, git refs)
   398→- `src/index.rs` — Index trait (ancestry, common ancestors)
   399→- `src/commit.rs` — Commit wrapper (parent_ids, change_id, description)
   400→- `src/backend.rs` — backend data types (CommitId, ChangeId, Signature)
   401→- `src/op_store.rs` — operation storage (commit_predecessors)
   402→- `src/operation.rs` — Operation type (predecessors_for_commit)
   403→
   404→## Show subcommand (0.18.0)
   405→
   406→### 0.18.0 — Initial show subcommand
   407→
   408→Implement `vc-x1 show` matching `jj show` output: commit/change IDs,
   409→bookmarks (local + remote), author/committer with timestamps,
   410→indented description, and file-level diff summary (Added/Modified/Removed).
   411→Uses `TreeDiffIterator` for sync tree diffing via jj-lib.
   412→
   413→### 0.18.1 — Flesh out show header
   414→
   415→Show header now has gitk-style fields: Ids, Author, Committer, Parent,
   416→Child, Branches (ancestry-based), Follows/Precedes (nearest tags),
   417→Description (body only). Supports `..` notation for multi-commit display
   418→with separators. File list: `-f` flag (default 50, `0`=none, `all`=unlimited)
   419→with first/last split when truncated. Remove `--indent` from desc,
   420→hardcode 4-space indent in both desc and show.
   421→
   422→### 0.19.0 — Unify `..` notation and CLI across all subcommands
   423→
   424→- Extract `collect_ids` in `common.rs` — returns `(Vec<CommitId>, usize)`,
   425→  ordered commit IDs and anchor index. Handles all `..` directions.
   426→- Extract `resolve_spec` — converts positional/flag args into `DotSpec`
   427→  (desc_count/anc_count) ready for `collect_ids`. Single code path for
   428→  all subcommands.
   429→- Replace `DotDirection` enum with `desc_count`/`anc_count` on `DotSpec`.
   430→- Remove `resolve_dot_args`, `ResolvedArgs`, `collect_both`/`collect_commit_lines`,
   431→  `show_both` — all replaced by `resolve_spec` + `collect_ids`.
   432→- All subcommands (chid, desc, list, show) now share one loop pattern.
   433→- `-r` flag supports `..` notation (e.g. `-r ..mpl -n 3`).
   434→- Rename `-l`/`--limit` to `-n`/`--commits` (`-l`/`--limit` kept as
   435→  hidden aliases). Positional `COMMITS` replaces `POS_COUNT`.
   436→- Count means total commits including anchor (e.g. `x.. 3` = x + 2
   437→  ancestors). Fixes off-by-one in descendants and bare-rev-with-count.
   438→- File list truncation: show first N files only. Skip truncation when
   439→  only 1 file would be omitted.
   440→- Raw jj revset syntax (`::`, `|`, `&`) no longer supported via `-r`;
   441→  use `..` notation instead.
   442→
   443→## Test dispersal and ochid list column
   444→
   445→### 0.20.1 — Disperse CLI parsing tests
   446→
   447→Move all CLI parsing tests from main.rs into per-subcommand test modules
   448→(chid.rs, desc.rs, list.rs, show.rs, finalize.rs). main.rs retains only
   449→the `unknown_command` test. Make `Commands` enum `pub(crate)` so submodule
   450→tests can match on it. Each module has a local `parse()` helper that
   451→returns the typed args struct directly, eliminating the `if let` boilerplate.
   452→
   453→### 0.21.0 — Show ochid in list output
   454→
   455→Replace commitID with ochid trailer value in `list` output. New column
   456→format: `chid  ochid  title` with 2-space gaps between columns. The ochid
   457→column is padded to `-w`/`--width` (default 21, fits `/.claude/` + 12-char
   458→changeID). Commits without an ochid trailer show a blank placeholder to
   459→keep columns aligned. Add `extract_ochid()` and `format_commit_with_ochid()`
   460→to common.rs. Also clean up CLI help: remove duplicate manual defaults from
   461→doc comments (clap shows `[default: ...]` automatically), use `default_value`
   462→for `--label`, and simplify `Header` enum from three variants to two
   463→(`Label(String)` and `None`).
   464→
   465→### 0.21.1 — Deduplicate common CLI flags
   466→
   467→Extract shared CLI args (pos_rev, pos_count, revision, repos, limit, label,
   468→no_label) into `CommonArgs` struct in common.rs using clap `#[command(flatten)]`.
   469→All four read-only subcommands (chid, desc, list, show) flatten it; list adds
   470→`width`, show adds `files`. Eliminates ~30 lines of duplicated arg definitions
   471→per subcommand. Tests updated to access fields via `.common`.
   472→
   473→### 0.22.0 — Add fix-ochid subcommand
   474→
   475→New `fix-ochid` subcommand to validate and fix ochid trailers in commit
   476→descriptions. Validates three properties: correct path prefix (derived from
   477→`--other-repo`), correct changeID length (`--id-len`, default 12), and that
   478→the ID resolves in the other repo. Supports `..` notation for revision ranges.
   479→Default is dry-run; `--no-dry-run` to actually write. `--fallback` provides a
   480→replacement value for IDs not found in the other repo (e.g. `/.claude/lost`
   481→for commits whose `.claude` history was lost to squashing). Optional `--title`
   482→to fix commit titles at the same time. Uses `jj describe --ignore-immutable`
   483→to rewrite descriptions.
   484→
   485→## Git commit headers and jj change-id preservation
   486→
   487→### How jj stores change IDs in git
   488→
   489→jj stores the change ID as a non-standard git commit header called
   490→`change-id`. This is part of the raw commit object, visible with
   491→`git cat-file commit <hash>`:
   492→
   493→```
   494→$ git cat-file commit f22d6c45b9d9
   495→tree 55d1626ba2ad932a568fd669d18cc19eb38510fa
   496→parent 432b7494734a5bbe549cd796335d9e67756f07bc
   497→author Wink Saville <wink@saville.com> 1773950107 -0700
   498→committer Wink Saville <wink@saville.com> 1773950107 -0700
   499→change-id mkoooktqrrqpllrkwxvosunuywplqsyq
   500→
   501→Deduplicate common CLI flags with #[command(flatten)] (0.21.1)
   502→
   503→Extract CommonArgs struct in common.rs shared by chid, desc, list, show.
   504→List adds width, show adds files on top. Update tests to access via .common.
   505→
   506→ochid: /.claude/lwwsmvroqzly
   507→```
   508→
   509→The `change-id` header sits alongside standard git headers (tree, parent,
   510→author, committer). The value is the full reverse-hex encoding of the
   511→change ID — jj's short form (`mkoooktqrrqp`) is a 12-char prefix of
   512→the full value (`mkoooktqrrqpllrkwxvosunuywplqsyq`).
   513→
   514→### Preservation guarantees
   515→
   516→From the [jj Git compatibility docs](https://jj-vcs.github.io/jj/latest/git-compatibility/):
   517→
   518→> Change IDs are stored in git commit headers as reverse hex encodings.
   519→> This is a non-standard header and is not preserved by all git tooling.
   520→> For example, the header is preserved by a `git commit --amend`, but is
   521→> not preserved through a rebase operation. GitHub and other major forges
   522→> seem to preserve them for the most part.
   523→
   524→**What preserves change IDs:**
   525→- `git push` / `git fetch` / `git clone` — the header is part of the
   526→  commit object, transferred as-is
   527→- `git commit --amend` — preserves non-standard headers
   528→- GitHub / major forges — preserve them in storage and API
   529→
   530→**What can strip change IDs:**
   531→- `git rebase` — rewrites commits, drops non-standard headers
   532→- `git cherry-pick` — creates new commits without the header
   533→- GitHub merge-squash / rebase-merge — creates new commits server-side
   534→
   535→### Configuration
   536→
   537→Controlled by `git.write-change-id-header` (default `true` since jj 0.29.0).
   538→Verify with:
   539→
   540→```
   541→$ jj config get git.write-change-id-header
   542→true
   543→```
   544→
   545→### Implications for ochid cross-references
   546→
   547→Since our ochid trailers reference change IDs in the *other* repo, the
   548→cross-references are only as stable as the change-id headers in both repos.
   549→A regular `git push` to GitHub and `git clone` back will preserve them,
   550→but any git tooling that rewrites commits could break the chain.
   551→
```

     1→# CLAUDE.md - Bot Instructions for hw-jjg-bot
     2→
     3→## Project Structure
     4→
     5→This project uses **two separate jj-git repos**:
     6→
     7→1. **App repo** (`/` — project root): Contains the application source code.
     8→2. **Bot session repo** (`/.claude/`): Contains Claude Code session data.
     9→
    10→Both repos are managed with `jj` (Jujutsu), which coexists with git.
    11→
    12→## Repo Paths (relative from project root)
    13→
    14→- App repo: `.` (project root)
    15→- Bot session repo: `.claude`
    16→  (symlink from `~/.claude/projects/<path-to-project-root>/.claude`)
    17→
    18→## Committing
    19→
    20→Use `-R` (`--repository`) at the end to target the correct repo. Use
    21→relative paths to reduce noise. Putting `-R` last keeps the verb/action
    22→visible at the start of the command.
    23→
    24→### App repo
    25→```
    26→jj commit -m "title" -m "body" -R .
    27→```
    28→
    29→### Bot session repo
    30→```
    31→jj commit -m "title" -m "body" -R .claude
    32→```
    33→
    34→## jj Basics
    35→
    36→- `jj st -R .` / `jj st -R .claude` — show working copy status
    37→- `jj log -R .` / `jj log -R .claude` — show commit log
    38→- `jj commit -m "title" -m "body" -R <repo>` — finalize working copy into a commit
    39→- `jj describe -m "title" -m "body" -R <repo>` — set description without committing
    40→- `jj git push --bookmark <name> -R <repo>` — push a bookmark (no
    41→  `--allow-new` flag; jj pushes new bookmarks without special flags)
    42→- In jj, the working copy (@) is always a mutable commit being edited.
    43→  `jj commit` finalizes it and creates a new empty working copy on top.
    44→- The `.claude` repo always has uncommitted changes during an active
    45→  session because session data updates continuously.
    46→
    47→## Pre-commit Requirements
    48→
    49→### User approval
    50→
    51→Never execute commit commands without the user's explicit approval.
    52→Present the full commands for review first; only run them after the
    53→user confirms.
    54→
    55→### Notes references
    56→
    57→Multiple references must be separated: `[2],[3]` not `[2,3]` or `[2][3]`.
    58→See [Todo format](notes/README.md#todo-format) for details.
    59→
    60→### Versioning
    61→
    62→Every change must start with a version bump. See
    63→[Versioning during development](notes/README.md#versioning-during-development)
    64→for details. Get user approval on single-step vs multi-step before starting.
    65→
    66→### Pre-commit checklist
    67→
    68→Before proposing a commit, run all of the following and fix any issues:
    69→
    70→1. `cargo fmt`
    71→2. `cargo clippy`
    72→3. `cargo test`
    73→4. `cargo install --path .` (if applicable)
    74→5. Retest after install
    75→
    76→## ochid Trailers
    77→
    78→Every commit body must include an `ochid:` trailer pointing to the
    79→counterpart commit in the other repo. The value is a workspace-root-relative
    80→path followed by the changeID:
    81→
    82→- App repo commits point to `.claude`: `ochid: /.claude/<changeID>`
    83→- Bot session commits point to app repo: `ochid: /<changeID>`
    84→
    85→Use `vc-x1 chid -R .,.claude -L` to get both changeIDs (first line
    86→is app repo, second is `.claude`).
    87→
    88→## Session End Workflows
    89→
    90→When the user asks to "commit both repos" or says they're done, commit
    91→both repos. Use the **same title** for both commits so they're easy to
    92→correlate. The body can differ: the app repo body should summarize code
    93→changes; the bot session repo body should note what was done in the
    94→session.
    95→
    96→```
    97→jj commit -m "shared title" -m "app body" -R .
    98→jj commit -m "shared title" -m "session body" -R .claude
    99→```
   100→
   101→After both commits, **pause and ask for user approval** before
   102→proceeding to bookmark/push/finalize.
   103→
   104→When the user also asks to push, advance the current bookmark on both
   105→repos, then push the app repo. Do **not** push `.claude` here —
   106→`finalize` handles that push after squashing trailing writes.
   107→
   108→Replace `<bookmark>` with the active bookmark (e.g. `main`,
   109→`dev-0.14.0`).
   110→
   111→```
   112→jj bookmark set <bookmark> -r @- -R .
   113→jj bookmark set <bookmark> -r @- -R .claude
   114→jj git push --bookmark <bookmark> -R .
   115→```
   116→
   117→### Late changes after push
   118→
   119→If changes are made to the app repo after it has been pushed (e.g.
   120→updating CLAUDE.md or memory), the commit is now immutable. Use
   121→`--ignore-immutable` to squash the changes in, then re-push:
   122→
   123→```
   124→jj squash --ignore-immutable -R .
   125→jj bookmark set <bookmark> -r @- -R .
   126→jj git push --bookmark <bookmark> -R .
   127→```
   128→
   129→### Finalize the .claude repo
   130→
   131→The **very last action** in a session is to finalize the `.claude` repo.
   132→This squashes the working copy into the session commit and pushes. The
   133→delay gives a safety margin against any pending writes. Always use a
   134→short relative path for `--repo`.
   135→
   136→**Nothing should happen after finalize** — no memory writes, no tool
   137→calls, no additional output. If any work is done after finalize, run
   138→finalize again so the trailing writes are captured.
   139→
   140→`--bookmark` is required — use the active bookmark for the session.
   141→
   142→```
   143→vc-x1 finalize --repo .claude --bookmark <bookmark> --delay 10 --detach --push
   144→```
   145→
   146→Do **not** echo or restate the finalize output — the Bash tool
   147→already displays it. Any trailing text output creates writes that
   148→miss the finalize squash window.
   149→
```

wink@3900x 26-03-20T16:31:32.405Z:~/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/.claude ((main))

> AGENT

The bug: `fix_ochid_in_description` can only *truncate* IDs, not *extend* them. When the ochid has an 8-char ID and `id_len` is 12, the code does `bare_id[..min(8, 12)]` which is still 8 chars. So `jj describe` gets the same description and says "Nothing changed."

The fix: when the ID is too short, resolve it in the other repo to get the full change ID, then take the first `id_len` chars. This needs to happen in the `fix_ochid` function (which has repo access), not in `fix_ochid_in_description` (which is pure string manipulation).

Want me to go ahead with this as 0.22.2?

> AGENT

So `resolve_revset` returns `Vec<CommitId>`. From the commit ID I can get the commit and then its change ID. The simplest approach: resolve the bare ID in the other repo, get the full change hex, and pass the first `id_len` chars as a `resolved_id` parameter.

> TOOL

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/src/fix_ochid.rs",
  "old_string": "/// Fix the ochid trailer in a commit description.\nfn fix_ochid_in_description(\n    desc: &str,\n    other_prefix: &str,\n    id_len: usize,\n    new_title: Option<&str>,\n) -> String {\n    let mut lines: Vec<String> = desc.lines().map(|l| l.to_string()).collect();\n\n    // Fix title if requested\n    if let Some(title) = new_title\n        && !lines.is_empty()\n    {\n        lines[0] = title.to_string();\n    }\n\n    // Fix ochid trailer\n    for line in &mut lines {\n        let trimmed = line.trim();\n        if let Some(value) = trimmed.strip_prefix(\"ochid:\") {\n            let value = value.trim();\n            // Extract the bare changeID (strip any existing path prefix)\n            let bare_id = if let Some(pos) = value.rfind('/') {\n                &value[pos + 1..]\n            } else {\n                value\n            };\n            // Normalize to id_len chars\n            let short_id = &bare_id[..bare_id.len().min(id_len)];\n            *line = format!(\"ochid: {other_prefix}{short_id}\");\n        }\n    }",
  "new_string": "/// Fix the ochid trailer in a commit description.\n///\n/// If `resolved_id` is provided, it replaces the bare changeID entirely\n/// (used when the existing ID is too short to extend by truncation alone).\nfn fix_ochid_in_description(\n    desc: &str,\n    other_prefix: &str,\n    id_len: usize,\n    new_title: Option<&str>,\n    resolved_id: Option<&str>,\n) -> String {\n    let mut lines: Vec<String> = desc.lines().map(|l| l.to_string()).collect();\n\n    // Fix title if requested\n    if let Some(title) = new_title\n        && !lines.is_empty()\n    {\n        lines[0] = title.to_string();\n    }\n\n    // Fix ochid trailer\n    for line in &mut lines {\n        let trimmed = line.trim();\n        if let Some(value) = trimmed.strip_prefix(\"ochid:\") {\n            let value = value.trim();\n            let id = if let Some(rid) = resolved_id {\n                &rid[..rid.len().min(id_len)]\n            } else {\n                // Extract the bare changeID (strip any existing path prefix)\n                let bare_id = if let Some(pos) = value.rfind('/') {\n                    &value[pos + 1..]\n                } else {\n                    value\n                };\n                // Normalize to id_len chars\n                &bare_id[..bare_id.len().min(id_len)]\n            };\n            *line = format!(\"ochid: {other_prefix}{id}\");\n        }\n    }"
}
```

```
   442→    fn fix_title_and_ochid() {
   443→        let desc = "Old title\n\nochid: bare12345678\n";
   444→        let result = fix_ochid_in_description(desc, "/.claude/", DEFAULT_ID_LEN, Some("New title"));
   445→        assert!(result.starts_with("New title\n"));
   446→        assert!(result.contains("ochid: /.claude/bare12345678"));
   447→    }
   448→
   449→    #[test]
   450→    fn no_ochid_no_change() {
   451→        let desc = "Title\n\nNo trailer here.\n";
   452→        let result = fix_ochid_in_description(desc, "/.claude/", DEFAULT_ID_LEN, None);
   453→        assert_eq!(result, desc);
   454→    }
   455→
   456→    #[test]
   457→    fn other_path_without_trailing_slash() {
   458→        let desc = "Title\n\nochid: abcdefghijkl\n";
   459→        let result = fix_ochid_in_description(desc, "/", DEFAULT_ID_LEN, None);
   460→        assert!(result.contains("ochid: /abcdefghijkl"));
   461→    }
   462→

   466→        let result = fix_ochid_in_description(desc, "/", 8, None);
   467→        assert!(result.contains("ochid: /abcdefgh"));
   468→        assert!(!result.contains("ijkl"));
   469→    }
```

```
     1→use std::path::PathBuf;
     2→use std::process::Command;
     3→use std::sync::Arc;
     4→
     5→use clap::Args;
     6→use jj_lib::object_id::ObjectId;
     7→use jj_lib::repo::{ReadonlyRepo, Repo};
     8→use jj_lib::workspace::Workspace;
     9→
    10→use crate::common;
    11→use crate::toml_simple;
    12→
    13→const DEFAULT_ID_LEN: usize = 12;
    14→const VC_CONFIG_FILE: &str = ".vc-config.toml";
    15→
    16→#[derive(Args, Debug)]
    17→pub struct FixOchidArgs {
    18→    /// Revision (with optional .. notation)
    19→    #[arg(value_name = "REVISION")]
    20→    pub pos_rev: Option<String>,
    21→
    22→    /// Number of commits (per dotted side)
    23→    #[arg(value_name = "COMMITS")]
    24→    pub pos_count: Option<usize>,
    25→
    26→    /// Revision(s) to fix
    27→    #[arg(short, long, default_value = "@")]
    28→    pub revision: String,
    29→
    30→    /// Path to jj repo
    31→    #[arg(short = 'R', long, default_value = ".")]
    32→    pub repo: PathBuf,
    33→
    34→    /// Path to the other repo (e.g. .claude or .)
    35→    #[arg(long = "other-repo")]
    36→    pub other_repo: PathBuf,
    37→
    38→    /// Expected changeID length
    39→    #[arg(long = "id-len", default_value_t = DEFAULT_ID_LEN)]
    40→    pub id_len: usize,
    41→
    42→    /// New title to replace the first line (optional)
    43→    #[arg(long)]
    44→    pub title: Option<String>,
    45→
    46→    /// Fallback ochid value for IDs not found in other repo (e.g. /.claude/lost)
    47→    #[arg(long)]
    48→    pub fallback: Option<String>,
    49→
    50→    /// Actually write changes (default is dry-run)
    51→    #[arg(long = "no-dry-run")]
    52→    pub no_dry_run: bool,
    53→
    54→    /// Number of commits to fix
    55→    #[arg(short = 'n', long = "commits", value_name = "COMMITS")]
    56→    pub limit: Option<usize>,
    57→}
    58→
    59→/// Derive the ochid prefix from the other repo's `.vc-config.toml`.
    60→///
    61→/// The `workspace.path` value is the repo's path relative to the workspace
    62→/// root, which is exactly the ochid prefix (with a trailing `/` when the
    63→/// path isn't just `/`).
    64→fn ochid_prefix_from_config(
    65→    config: &std::collections::HashMap<String, String>,
    66→) -> Result<String, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
    67→    let ws_path = toml_simple::toml_get(config, "workspace.path")
    68→        .ok_or("missing workspace.path in .vc-config.toml")?;
    69→    let trimmed = ws_path.trim_end_matches('/');
    70→    if trimmed.is_empty() {
    71→        Ok("/".to_string())
    72→    } else {
    73→        Ok(format!("{trimmed}/"))
    74→    }
    75→}
    76→
    77→/// Problems found with an ochid trailer.
    78→#[derive(Debug)]
    79→struct OchidIssues {
    80→    wrong_prefix: bool,
    81→    wrong_length: bool,
    82→    not_found: bool,
    83→}
    84→
    85→impl OchidIssues {
    86→    fn any(&self) -> bool {
    87→        self.wrong_prefix || self.wrong_length || self.not_found
    88→    }
    89→
    90→    fn summary(&self) -> String {
    91→        let mut parts = Vec::new();
    92→        if self.wrong_prefix {
    93→            parts.push("wrong prefix");
    94→        }
    95→        if self.wrong_length {
    96→            parts.push("wrong ID length");
    97→        }
    98→        if self.not_found {
    99→            parts.push("ID not found in other repo");
   100→        }
   101→        parts.join(", ")
   102→    }
   103→}
   104→
   105→/// Validate an ochid trailer value against expected prefix, length, and other repo.
   106→fn validate_ochid(
   107→    value: &str,
   108→    other_prefix: &str,
   109→    id_len: usize,
   110→    other_workspace: &Workspace,
   111→    other_repo: &Arc<ReadonlyRepo>,
   112→) -> OchidIssues {
   113→    let wrong_prefix = !value.starts_with(other_prefix);
   114→
   115→    // Extract the bare ID
   116→    let bare_id = if let Some(pos) = value.rfind('/') {
   117→        &value[pos + 1..]
   118→    } else {
   119→        value
   120→    };
   121→
   122→    let wrong_length = bare_id.len() != id_len;
   123→
   124→    // Check if the ID resolves in the other repo
   125→    let not_found = common::resolve_revset(other_workspace, other_repo, bare_id)
   126→        .map(|ids| ids.is_empty())
   127→        .unwrap_or(true);
   128→
   129→    OchidIssues {
   130→        wrong_prefix,
   131→        wrong_length,
   132→        not_found,
   133→    }
   134→}
   135→
   136→/// Fix the ochid trailer in a commit description.
   137→///
   138→/// If `resolved_id` is provided, it replaces the bare changeID entirely
   139→/// (used when the existing ID is too short to extend by truncation alone).
   140→fn fix_ochid_in_description(
   141→    desc: &str,
   142→    other_prefix: &str,
   143→    id_len: usize,
   144→    new_title: Option<&str>,
   145→    resolved_id: Option<&str>,
   146→) -> String {
   147→    let mut lines: Vec<String> = desc.lines().map(|l| l.to_string()).collect();
   148→
   149→    // Fix title if requested
   150→    if let Some(title) = new_title
   151→        && !lines.is_empty()
   152→    {
   153→        lines[0] = title.to_string();
   154→    }
   155→
   156→    // Fix ochid trailer
   157→    for line in &mut lines {
   158→        let trimmed = line.trim();
   159→        if let Some(value) = trimmed.strip_prefix("ochid:") {
   160→            let value = value.trim();
   161→            let id = if let Some(rid) = resolved_id {
   162→                &rid[..rid.len().min(id_len)]
   163→            } else {
   164→                // Extract the bare changeID (strip any existing path prefix)
   165→                let bare_id = if let Some(pos) = value.rfind('/') {
   166→                    &value[pos + 1..]
   167→                } else {
   168→                    value
   169→                };
   170→                // Normalize to id_len chars
   171→                &bare_id[..bare_id.len().min(id_len)]
   172→            };
   173→            *line = format!("ochid: {other_prefix}{id}");
   174→        }
   175→    }
   176→
   177→    // Preserve trailing newline if original had one
   178→    let mut result = lines.join("\n");
   179→    if desc.ends_with('\n') {
   180→        result.push('\n');
   181→    }
   182→    result
   183→}
   184→
   185→pub fn fix_ochid(args: &FixOchidArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
   186→    let (workspace, repo) = common::load_repo(&args.repo)?;
   187→    let (other_workspace, other_repo) = common::load_repo(&args.other_repo)?;
   188→    let other_config = toml_simple::toml_load(&args.other_repo.join(VC_CONFIG_FILE))?;
   189→    let other_prefix = ochid_prefix_from_config(&other_config)?;
   190→
   191→    let spec = common::resolve_spec(
   192→        args.pos_rev.as_deref(),
   193→        args.pos_count,
   194→        &args.revision,
   195→        args.limit,
   196→        "@",
   197→    );
   198→
   199→    let (ids, _anchor_index) = common::collect_ids(
   200→        &workspace,
   201→        &repo,
   202→        &spec.rev,
   203→        spec.desc_count,
   204→        spec.anc_count,
   205→    )?;
   206→    if ids.is_empty() {
   207→        return Err(format!("no commits found for revision '{}'", args.revision).into());
   208→    }
   209→
   210→    let root_id = repo.store().root_commit_id().clone();
   211→    let mut fixed = 0;
   212→    let mut skipped = 0;
   213→    let mut errors = 0;
   214→
   215→    for commit_id in &ids {
   216→        if *commit_id == root_id {
   217→            continue;
   218→        }
   219→        let commit = repo.store().get_commit(commit_id)?;
   220→        let desc = commit.description();
   221→        let change_hex = jj_lib::hex_util::encode_reverse_hex(commit.change_id().as_bytes());
   222→        let change_short = &change_hex[..change_hex.len().min(12)];
   223→
   224→        // Extract current ochid value
   225→        let current_ochid = common::extract_ochid(&commit);
   226→
   227→        // Validate current ochid
   228→        let issues = if let Some(ref ochid_val) = current_ochid {
   229→            validate_ochid(
   230→                ochid_val,
   231→                &other_prefix,
   232→                args.id_len,
   233→                &other_workspace,
   234→                &other_repo,
   235→            )
   236→        } else {
   237→            // No ochid trailer — nothing to fix
   238→            skipped += 1;
   239→            if !args.no_dry_run {
   240→                println!("skip {change_short}  (no ochid trailer)");
   241→            }
   242→            continue;
   243→        };
   244→
   245→        if !issues.any() {
   246→            skipped += 1;
   247→            if !args.no_dry_run {
   248→                println!("ok   {change_short}  (valid)");
   249→            }
   250→            continue;
   251→        }
   252→
   253→        // Resolve the full change ID from the other repo when length is wrong
   254→        let resolved_id = if issues.wrong_length {
   255→            let ochid_val = current_ochid.as_deref().unwrap_or("");
   256→            let bare_id = if let Some(pos) = ochid_val.rfind('/') {
   257→                &ochid_val[pos + 1..]
   258→            } else {
   259→                ochid_val
   260→            };
   261→            if let Ok(commit_ids) = common::resolve_revset(&other_workspace, &other_repo, bare_id)
   262→                && let Some(cid) = commit_ids.first()
   263→            {
   264→                let other_commit = other_repo.store().get_commit(cid)?;
   265→                let full_hex =
   266→                    jj_lib::hex_util::encode_reverse_hex(other_commit.change_id().as_bytes());
   267→                Some(full_hex)
   268→            } else {
   269→                None
   270→            }
   271→        } else {
   272→            None
   273→        };
   274→
   275→        // Build the fixed description
   276→        let mut new_desc = fix_ochid_in_description(
   277→            desc,
   278→            &other_prefix,
   279→            args.id_len,
   280→            args.title.as_deref(),
   281→            resolved_id.as_deref(),
   282→        );
   283→
   284→        // Re-extract the fixed ochid value for post-validation
   285→        let mut fixed_ochid = {
   286→            let mut val = None;
   287→            for line in new_desc.lines() {
   288→                let trimmed = line.trim();
   289→                if let Some(v) = trimmed.strip_prefix("ochid:") {
   290→                    val = Some(v.trim().to_string());
   291→                }
   292→            }
   293→            val
   294→        };
   295→
   296→        let post_issues = if let Some(ref v) = fixed_ochid {
   297→            validate_ochid(v, &other_prefix, args.id_len, &other_workspace, &other_repo)
   298→        } else {
   299→            OchidIssues {
   300→                wrong_prefix: false,
   301→                wrong_length: false,
   302→                not_found: true,
   303→            }
   304→        };
   305→
   306→        if post_issues.not_found {
   307→            if let Some(ref fallback) = args.fallback {
   308→                // Replace the ochid line with the fallback value
   309→                let mut fb_lines: Vec<String> = new_desc.lines().map(|l| l.to_string()).collect();
   310→                for line in &mut fb_lines {
   311→                    if line.trim().starts_with("ochid:") {
   312→                        *line = format!("ochid: {fallback}");
   313→                    }
   314→                }
   315→                let fb_desc = if new_desc.ends_with('\n') {
   316→                    format!("{}\n", fb_lines.join("\n"))
   317→                } else {
   318→                    fb_lines.join("\n")
   319→                };
   320→                new_desc = fb_desc;
   321→                fixed_ochid = Some(fallback.clone());
   322→            } else {
   323→                errors += 1;
   324→                eprintln!(
   325→                    "err  {change_short}  ID not found in other repo (ochid: {})",
   326→                    fixed_ochid.as_deref().unwrap_or("?")
   327→                );
   328→                continue;
   329→            }
   330→        }
   331→
   332→        if !args.no_dry_run {
   333→            let first_line = new_desc.lines().next().unwrap_or("");
   334→            println!("fix  {change_short}  {first_line}  [{}]", issues.summary());
   335→            if let Some(ref v) = fixed_ochid {
   336→                println!("     -> ochid: {v}");
   337→            }
   338→        } else {
   339→            let status = Command::new("jj")
   340→                .arg("describe")
   341→                .arg("-m")
   342→                .arg(&new_desc)
   343→                .arg("-r")
   344→                .arg(commit_id.hex())
   345→                .arg("-R")
   346→                .arg(&args.repo)
   347→                .arg("--ignore-immutable")
   348→                .stdout(std::process::Stdio::null())
   349→                .stderr(std::process::Stdio::inherit())
   350→                .status()?;
   351→            if !status.success() {
   352→                return Err(format!(
   353→                    "jj describe failed for {} (exit {})",
   354→                    change_short,
   355→                    status.code().unwrap_or(-1)
   356→                )
   357→                .into());
   358→            }
   359→            let first_line = new_desc.lines().next().unwrap_or("");
   360→            println!("fixed {change_short}  {first_line}");
   361→        }
   362→        fixed += 1;
   363→    }
   364→
   365→    println!(
   366→        "\n{fixed} fixed, {skipped} skipped, {errors} errors (of {} total)",
   367→        ids.len()
   368→    );
   369→    if errors > 0 {
   370→        Err(format!("{errors} commit(s) could not be fixed").into())
   371→    } else {
   372→        Ok(())
   373→    }
   374→}
   375→
   376→#[cfg(test)]
   377→mod tests {
   378→    use super::*;
```

```
   219→fn format_timestamp(ts: &jj_lib::backend::Timestamp) -> String {
   220→    let millis = ts.timestamp.0;
   221→    let secs = millis / 1000;
   222→    let tz_minutes = ts.tz_offset;
   223→
   224→    let dt = chrono::DateTime::from_timestamp(secs, 0)
   225→        .unwrap_or_default()
   226→        .with_timezone(
   227→            &chrono::FixedOffset::east_opt(tz_minutes * 60).unwrap_or(chrono::Utc.fix()),
   228→        );
```

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/src/fix_ochid.rs",
  "old_string": "pub fn fix_ochid(args: &FixOchidArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {",
  "new_string": "/// Maximum timestamp difference (in milliseconds) for title-based matching.\nconst TIMESTAMP_TOLERANCE_MS: i64 = 60_000;\n\n/// Find a unique matching commit in the other repo by title and timestamp.\n///\n/// Returns the other commit's change ID (full hex) if exactly one commit\n/// in the other repo has the same first description line and a committer\n/// timestamp within `TIMESTAMP_TOLERANCE_MS` of the source commit.\nfn find_matching_commit(\n    commit: &jj_lib::commit::Commit,\n    other_workspace: &Workspace,\n    other_repo: &Arc<ReadonlyRepo>,\n) -> Result<Option<String>, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    let title = commit.description().lines().next().unwrap_or(\"\");\n    if title.is_empty() {\n        return Ok(None);\n    }\n    let src_millis = commit.committer().timestamp.timestamp.0;\n\n    // Search all commits in the other repo\n    let all_ids = common::resolve_revset(other_workspace, other_repo, \"all()\")?;\n    let root_id = other_repo.store().root_commit_id().clone();\n\n    let mut matches = Vec::new();\n    for cid in &all_ids {\n        if *cid == root_id {\n            continue;\n        }\n        let other_commit = other_repo.store().get_commit(cid)?;\n        let other_title = other_commit.description().lines().next().unwrap_or(\"\");\n        if other_title != title {\n            continue;\n        }\n        let other_millis = other_commit.committer().timestamp.timestamp.0;\n        if (src_millis - other_millis).abs() <= TIMESTAMP_TOLERANCE_MS {\n            let full_hex =\n                jj_lib::hex_util::encode_reverse_hex(other_commit.change_id().as_bytes());\n            matches.push(full_hex);\n        }\n    }\n\n    if matches.len() == 1 {\n        Ok(Some(matches.into_iter().next().unwrap()))\n    } else {\n        Ok(None)\n    }\n}\n\n/// Append an ochid trailer to a commit description.\nfn append_ochid_trailer(desc: &str, other_prefix: &str, change_id: &str, id_len: usize) -> String {\n    let short_id = &change_id[..change_id.len().min(id_len)];\n    let trailer = format!(\"ochid: {other_prefix}{short_id}\");\n\n    let mut result = desc.trim_end().to_string();\n    // Add blank line before trailer if body exists, otherwise just newline\n    if result.lines().count() > 1 {\n        result.push('\\n');\n    } else {\n        result.push_str(\"\\n\\n\");\n    }\n    result.push_str(&trailer);\n    result.push('\\n');\n    result\n}\n\npub fn fix_ochid(args: &FixOchidArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {"
}
```
